the third one: an independently supplied rate `gamma` could transform with
those units. A model admitting explicit shared time dependence would instead
leave a common function `gamma(t)` by the same pointwise argument. It is
outside the autonomous class stated here; no primordial clock is derived.

**Exact relative equivalence.** In the unforced held-support model, for any
reference solution and the same initial state at `t_0`,

\[
x_{\rm alt}(t)=x_{\rm rel}(t),\qquad
\theta_{\rm alt}(t)=\theta_{\rm rel}(t)+\gamma(t-t_0)\mathbf1
\]

is a solution of the alternative law throughout the same regular interval.
All edge gaps, native phase pressure, metric, storage and form trajectories
coincide. In particular `J_g*1=0` makes the phase-source derivative identical.
This is an exact transformation, not a small-step approximation or a claim
that arbitrary absolute-phase observations are redundant. An external phase
reference, forcing or event law must be transformed and admitted separately.

The [exact controls](../../tests/physics/test_relational_locality_selection.py)
exercise balanced stars, the common-clock null direction, root isolation and
the homogeneity distinction. The existing executor keeps `gamma=0` and its
current phase chambers; this theorem introduces no runtime mode or expanded
admission. Native pressure, joint storage and uniform primitive locality
remain constitutive premises. The coefficients, their physical justification
and primitive support origin are not selected by this result.

### 11.3 Joint edge storage is selected by the prescribed loss and locality

<a id="joint-storage-locality-classification"></a>

The preceding phase-cost classification assumed separate form and phase
storage. The following result tests that split rather than imposing it.
Retain the native form row, with fixed `e>0,w>0`, held independently variable
nonnegative capacities, the same structural clock, and every finite connected
simple unit graph on its full regular phase domain. There are no inputs or
events. Let one twice continuously differentiable cost satisfy

\[
\mathcal S(x,\theta)=\sum_{\{i,j\}\in E}
 W(x_i-x_j,\theta_i-\theta_j),\qquad
W(r,\delta+2\pi)=W(r,\delta),
\]

\[
W\ge0,\qquad W(0,0)=0,\qquad
W(-r,-\delta)=W(r,\delta).
\]

The last condition expresses undirected edge reversal. Neither separate
evenness nor either axis cost is assumed. Suppose there exists a continuous
phase row `F` with section 11.1's uniform induced-neighborhood information
contract, same-model capacity homogeneity

\[
F(x,\theta;\lambda\nu)=\lambda F(x,\theta;\nu),\qquad\lambda>0,
\]

and the specified exact balance

\[
\frac{d\mathcal S}{dt}=-e\sum_i\frac{\nu_i q_i^2}{d_i},
\qquad q=Bx,\qquad
\dot x_i=\nu_i\left(-e\frac{q_i}{d_i}+wg_i\right).
\]

Homogeneity holds with the cost and all constitutive coefficients fixed.
No individual zero-capacity phase condition, gradient-flow representation,
native-aligned storage metric or previously selected phase row is imposed.
Then one constant `beta>0` necessarily gives

\[
\boxed{W(r,\delta)=\frac12r^2+\beta(1-\cos\delta),\qquad
F_i=\frac w\beta\frac{\nu_i q_i}{H_i}.}
\]

Thus the stated class selects the separated storage and existing phase
completion together. The prescribed loss, native pressure, locality, capacity
homogeneity and edge-additive cost remain premises of this conditional result.

**1. A necessary condition at zero phase-storage gradient.** Write
`f=partial_r W` and `h=partial_delta W`. Simultaneous reversal makes both
functions odd under `(r,delta)->(-r,-delta)`; in particular `f(0,0)=h(0,0)=0`.
At a root with incident differences `(r_j,delta_j)`, define

\[
Q=\sum_jr_j,\quad P=\sum_j f(r_j,\delta_j),\quad
J=\sum_j h(r_j,\delta_j).
\]

Initially take each gap in `(-pi,pi)` and make the root regular by adding
aligned zero-form leaves if needed. Give only the root positive capacity.
For each neighbor `j`, add three private leaves: one with
`x_leaf=2*x_j-x_root` and `theta_leaf=2*theta_j-theta_root`, and two with
the neighbor's own form and phase. All these leaves have zero capacity.
The old incidence and its reflected counterpart cancel `h` at `j`; the
aligned incidences contribute zero. Every original neighbor therefore has
zero phase-storage gradient. Its relative phase resultant is
`2*(1+cos(delta_j))>0`, and all private leaves have nonantipodal degree-one
phase data. The completed finite tree is regular everywhere.

Each private leaf sees only zero capacities in its admitted neighborhood.
Scaling the sole active root capacity leaves those local data unchanged,
while capacity homogeneity would scale its phase velocity. Consequently its
phase row is zero. This uses locality and homogeneity, not the unassumed rule
that any node with zero own capacity must freeze. All nonroot form velocities
also vanish. Global balance therefore reduces, whenever `J=0`, to

\[
\boxed{P\left(e\frac Qd-wg\right)=e\frac{Q^2}{d}.}
\tag{JS1}
\]

The phase row at the root cancels from this identity. Thus it is necessary
for every candidate completion, regardless of its dependence on neighboring
capacities. The auxiliary vertices are comparison states, not live support
events or a proposed mechanism of formation.

**2. Aligned padding resolves the native resultant.** For a finite collection
with `J=0`, put `X=sum_j cos(delta_j)`, `Y=sum_j sin(delta_j)` and let `d` be
its size. Append `m` additional incidences with `(r,delta)=(0,0)`. They change
neither `P,Q,J,Y` nor any cost derivative, but the degree becomes `d+m` and
the real resultant becomes `X+m`. For all sufficiently large integers `m`,
the root is regular with

\[
g=-\frac1\pi\arctan\frac{Y}{X+m}.
\]

Constructing the preceding completion for each padded star gives, with
`k=w/pi>0`,

\[
P\left[eQ+k(d+m)\arctan\frac{Y}{X+m}\right]=eQ^2.
\tag{JS2}
\]

If `Q!=0`, positivity of `e` forces `P!=0`, so
`(d+m)*arctan(Y/(X+m))` must be independent of `m`. Yet

\[
(d+m)\arctan\frac{Y}{X+m}
 =Y+\frac{(d-X)Y}{m}+O(m^{-2}).
\]

Here `d-X=sum_j(1-cos(delta_j))>=0`, and it is strictly positive if `Y!=0`.
A constant sequence has its limiting value `Y`, so its displayed first
correction must vanish. It follows that `Y=0`, and (JS2) then gives `P=Q`.
Only integer padding counts are used; no weighted-graph limit or uniform
bound on the auxiliary phase velocities is assumed.

**3. Derive an aligned form anchor.** Fix any `r!=0`. The periodic smooth
function `delta->W(r,delta)` has a stationary point away from the single
excluded antipodal phase. Indeed, a nonconstant periodic function attains
distinct minimum and maximum values, so at least one extremum is
nonantipodal; a constant function has every point stationary. Apply step 2
to that singleton incidence. Since `Q=r!=0`, its sine must vanish, so its
nonantipodal phase is zero. Moreover `P=Q` gives

\[
h(r,0)=0,\qquad f(r,0)=r.
\]

Continuity includes `r=0`; integrating with `W(0,0)=0` proves
`W(r,0)=r^2/2`. These axis properties have been derived, not assumed.

Now consider any finite collection with `J=0`, including one with `Q=0`.
Append a single aligned incidence `(a,0)`, choosing `a` so that `Q+a!=0`.
Its contributions are `h=0`, `f=a` and zero sine. Step 2 applies and gives
`Y=0` and `P+a=Q+a`. Consequently every such collection satisfies

\[
\sum_jh(r_j,\delta_j)=0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\sum_j\sin\delta_j=0,\qquad
\sum_jf(r_j,\delta_j)=\sum_jr_j.
\tag{JS3}
\]

**4. Classify the phase derivative without assuming separability.** The image
`I` of the continuous function `h` on the connected domain
`R times (-pi,pi)` is an interval, symmetric about zero by simultaneous
reversal. It is not the singleton `{0}`: that would contradict (JS3) for
a single gap with nonzero sine. If two states have the same value of `h`,
apply (JS3) to the first and the reversal of the second. Their sines agree.
There is therefore a well-defined bounded function

\[
T:I\longrightarrow[-1,1],\qquad T(h(r,\delta))=\sin\delta.
\]

Balanced triples in (JS3) give `T(a+b)=T(a)+T(b)` whenever
`a,b,a+b` belong to `I`. This bounded additive function is linear. To see
the needed regularity directly, choose `[-eta,eta]` inside `I`. For any
positive integer `n` and sufficiently small `t`, repeated addition gives
`n*T(t)=T(n*t)`, hence `|T(t)|<=1/n`. Thus `T` is continuous at zero;
rational addition and subdivision extend linearity across `I`.
Its slope cannot be zero, since nonzero sine values occur. Accordingly,
for one nonzero constant `beta`,

\[
h(r,\delta)=\beta\sin\delta.
\]

Integrating in phase and using the derived form axis gives
`W(r,delta)=r^2/2+beta*(1-cos(delta))`. Continuity and periodicity include
antipodal endpoints. Nonnegativity at `r=0` forces `beta>=0`; the nonzero
slope excludes `beta=0`, so `beta>0`.

**5. Select the phase completion.** For this now-derived storage, section
[11.2](#primitive-locality-phase-clock) applies to the candidate phase law:
global balance and the uniform information contract leave only
`F=F_rel+gamma*1`. The reference is homogeneous in capacity. Keeping all
coefficients fixed, homogeneity of the candidate then requires
`gamma=lambda*gamma` for every positive `lambda`, so `gamma=0`.
Conversely, the existing reference storage and phase row satisfy all the
stated requirements. No new default law or runtime parameter is introduced.

**Scope and remaining premises.** The prescribed exact loss is essential:
its positive quadratic form term supplies the nonzero-form restriction in
(JS2), and positive phase coupling supplies the padding constraint. Neither
`e=0` nor `w=0` is covered. The argument uses arbitrary support sizes and
degrees, independent capacities, and the full regular phase domain; a fixed
P2, a fixed degree family, or acute-only observations do not supply these
quantifiers. In particular the periodic extremum step cannot silently be
restricted to acute gaps. Nonadditive storage, different losses, additional
state variables, inputs and events require separate admission.

The arbitrary comparison leaves do not derive support, capacities or a
physical clock. The positive coefficient `beta` remains free and is the
existing storage-scale parameter. This theorem does not establish physical
energy or the uniquely correct pressure of nature. Its normalization also
retains a boundary: a constant per edge is excluded by `W(0,0)=0`; without
that convention it is invisible to fixed-support evolution but affects
support-event storage jumps. The older auxiliary-potential and cotangent
models retain their different laws and balance premises.

**Why smaller checks do not select the cost.** At consensus let the edge
Hessian have entries `(a,b;b,c)`, with `c>0`, and put `k=w/pi`.
For `t<e/k`, set `sigma=e/(e-k*t)`, `b=c*t` and `a=sigma+c*t^2`.
The positive quadratic tangent storage then has the mixed form
`sigma*E_D(x)+c*E_D(theta+t*x)`. With `p=B*theta`, the local tangent rows

\[
\dot x=-ND^{-1}(eq+kp),\qquad
\dot\theta=ND^{-1}\big[(k\sigma/c+et)q+ktp\big]
\]

satisfy the prescribed quadratic loss exactly. This uses a local real phase
lift, not a globally periodic storage. The periodic candidate
`W=sigma*r^2/2+c*(1-cos(delta+t*r))` has that Hessian but need not satisfy
the nonlinear balance: at `e=w=1/2`, `c=1`, `t=-pi`, `r=-2/3`, `delta=pi/3`
and total two-node capacity `3`, its phase derivative vanishes while
`Sdot=-1/6` and required `-L=-2/3`. No finite phase completion can supply
the missing work at that state.

Another candidate `W=r^2/2+(beta+alpha*r^2)*(1-cos(delta))`, with
`beta>0, alpha>0`, passes the regular two-node phase-critical check.
On a six-cycle with phase gaps `pi/3` and alternating form `+a,-a`, however,
both native phase pressure and the entire phase-storage gradient vanish.
Its form-storage gradient is `(1+alpha)*q`, so `Sdot=-(1+alpha)*L`,
independently of the phase row. This violates the specified equality even
though storage decreases at that state. Exact loss and passivity are distinct
conditions; this example alone does not give a complete passive alternative.

The [joint-storage controls](../../tests/physics/test_relational_joint_storage.py)
check the reflected completion, aligned-padding restriction and named mixed
counterexamples with independent exact arithmetic. A compatible mixed
quadratic tangent law shows why a consensus expansion alone cannot establish
this theorem; its natural periodic continuation can fail even on an acute
two-node state. A form-dependent phase-cost amplitude can pass a two-node
critical-point check and fail on a phase-balanced cycle. These are controls
of the proof's obligations, not a substitute for its all-state argument.
The existing [phase-law locality controls](../../tests/physics/test_relational_locality_selection.py)
and [engine execution tests](../../tests/test_relational_exchange_execution.py)
retain the continuous-law versus represented-evaluation boundary.

### 11.4 Passive phase loss is a distinct admissible completion

<a id="relational-passive-loss-completion"></a>

The exact loss in section 11.3 is an independent premise. To examine it,
**retain the already specified comparison storage**
`E=E_D+beta*V_phi`, rather than asserting that its classification still holds
after weakening that premise. On the same unforced held-support regular
domain, with `e,w,beta>0` and held `N>=0`, declare the complete family

\[
\dot x=N(-eD^{-1}Bx+wg),\qquad
F_\rho=\dot\theta=\frac w\beta H^{-1}NBx+\rho Ng,
\qquad \rho\ge0.
\]

Here `rho` is a supplied constant coefficient, not a diagnostic, an inferred
reservoir or a consequence of the nodal product. The case `rho=0` is the
existing reference; other values are comparison laws, not production modes.
The added row uses only primitive neighbors and own capacity. It is linear
in capacity and preserves common form shifts, common phase rotations,
relabeling and simultaneous `(x,theta)->(-x,-theta)` reversal. It also
vanishes at a node with zero capacity.

The older [passive-transfer comparison](../TNFR_VARIATIONAL_PRINCIPLE.md#1318-source-sensitivity-joint-work-and-the-passive-transfer-limit)
already used an additional multiple of `g`. That result employs centered
Euclidean form storage and unit capacity on a supplied prism. Its cancellation
method is reused here; its storage, rates and numerical bounds are not
transferred to this Dirichlet-storage family.

**Exact work and unchanged form equation.** Since `grad(V_phi)=-H*g`,

\[
\boxed{\dot E=-L-R_\rho,\qquad
L=e\sum_i\frac{\nu_iq_i^2}{d_i},\qquad
R_\rho=\beta\rho\sum_i\nu_iH_i g_i^2\ge0.}
\]

Thus passivity, locality, capacity homogeneity and the listed symmetries do
not select the exact reference loss. The extra phase motion dissipates
storage directly whenever `rho>0` and `Ng!=0`; the reference transfers
phase/form work before dissipation through form contrast. This accounting
does not identify either loss with a physical bath or make it available to
fund an event.

The complete form-mean formula is still

\[
\dot{\bar x}=\frac1n\mathbf1^TN(-eD^{-1}q+wg).
\]

It agrees at the same prepared state, but its later values generally differ
because the phase trajectory changes. The mean angular velocity acquires
`rho*mean(Ng)`; it is not generally a common rotation. Likewise the pressure
derivative remains `pdot=-eD^-1B*xdot+w*J_g*F_rho`, with the chosen phase row
included. No additional term has been silently inserted into `xdot`.

**Units and replicas.** The new coefficient obeys
`[rho*nu_f]=1/[time]` for dimensionless angular phase and native `g`.
In section 4's convention, capacity has inverse-clock units and `rho` is
dimensionless. Under `x_new=a*x`, `t_new=b*t`, `N_new=N/b`, it stays unchanged.
If capacity is held numerically fixed instead, `rho_new=rho/b`. When the
pressure weights are renormalized with `k=e+a*w` and `N_new=k*N/b`, the
equivalent coefficient is `rho_new=rho/k`. Failing to transform it changes
the comparison law. The ratio `rho*sqrt(beta)/w` is an additional invariant
under these form/clock conventions; their covariance does not choose it.
Equal complete replicas retain `g` and each prepared capacity, so the added
row descends under the same supplied synchronized replication as the reference.
This is a compatibility check, not a derivation of `rho`.

**Equilibria and inactive nodes.** For every held nonnegative capacity vector,
both laws have precisely the equilibrium conditions

\[
Nq=0,\qquad Ng=0.
\]

At an equilibrium the nonnegative losses must vanish. Since `e>0`, this gives
`Nq=0`; the form equation then gives `Ng=0`. Conversely these two conditions
annul both complete rows. With all capacities positive, connectedness gives
uniform form and a regular critical point of phase storage, which can include
winding patterns. Zero capacities retain inactive frozen coordinates and do
not permit replacing these conditions by global consensus. Equal equilibria
do not imply equal trajectories, attraction basins or rates.

**One prepared analytic discriminator.** On P2 set `e=w=1/2`, `beta=1`,
capacities `(1,2)`, uniform form, and the phase gap
`theta_0-theta_1=pi/3`. Both nodes lie strictly inside the acute domain. Then

\[
g=(-1/3,1/3),\qquad H_0=H_1=3\sqrt3/2,\qquad
\dot x=(-1/6,1/3).
\]

The reference initially has `F_rel=0` and `Edot=0`. Relative to it, the declared
alternative predicts

\[
\Delta F=(-\rho/3,2\rho/3),\qquad
\Delta(\dot\theta_0-\dot\theta_1)=-\rho,\qquad
R_\rho=\rho\sqrt3/2,
\]

\[
\Delta\ddot x=wN J_g\Delta F
 =\left(\frac{\rho}{2\pi},-\frac{\rho}{\pi}\right),
\qquad
J_g=\frac1\pi\begin{pmatrix}-1&1\\1&-1\end{pmatrix}.
\]

The initial form response is identical; the relative phase velocity, storage
loss and form acceleration distinguish the laws. These are exact conditional
initial-rate predictions, not a sampled response, a fitted coefficient or a
physical validation. No previously frozen acquisition is rerun or reinterpreted.

**Local recovery survives with different damping.** Take a strictly acute
phase equilibrium, uniform form and positive held capacities. Let
`K=Hess(V_phi)` and evaluate `H` at that equilibrium. For form/phase
perturbations `(u,v)`, the linearization is

\[
\begin{aligned}
\dot u&=-eND^{-1}Bu-wNH^{-1}Kv,\\
\dot v&=(w/\beta)NH^{-1}Bu-\rho NH^{-1}Kv.
\end{aligned}
\]

On the quotient by the two common offsets,
`Q=(u^TBu+beta*v^TKv)/2` is positive definite and

\[
\dot Q=-e(Bu)^TND^{-1}(Bu)
       -\beta\rho(Kv)^TNH^{-1}(Kv).
\]

For `rho>0` this is strictly negative on every nonzero quotient state, so the
linearization is Hurwitz and the smooth nonlinear law has local exponential
recovery. The `rho=0` case uses the reference's separately proved exchange
observability argument. This establishes existence of a local recovery
neighborhood; it does not preserve a previous numerical rate or capture tube.

At phase consensus with common capacity `nu`, a nonzero random-walk
Laplacian mode `lambda` has characteristic polynomial

\[
s^2+\nu\lambda(e+\rho/\pi)s
+\nu^2\lambda^2\left(\frac{e\rho}{\pi}
  +\frac{w^2}{\beta\pi^2}\right)=0.
\]

The reference polynomial is recovered at zero `rho`; extra dissipation changes
both coefficients. For the explicit comparison `e=w=1/2`, `beta=1`, its
discriminant divided by `(nu*lambda)^2` is `1/4-1/pi^2>0` at `rho=0`, but
`1/4-1/pi<0` at `rho=1`. Thus the same equilibrium and local-recovery verdict
coexist with real decaying modes in one law and damped oscillatory modes in
the other. This is a tangent response distinction, not a perpetual nonlinear
pulse or a change in the supplied graph. On an acute uniform cycle twist,
replace the last `w^2/(beta*pi^2)` term by `w^2/(beta*pi^2*cos(kappa))`.
No monotonic optimal decay claim, global attraction, zero-capacity recovery
or unchanged validated formation/capture evidence follows from these tangent
formulas.

**A sufficient selection premise under total passivity.** One can recover the
reference without assuming its exact loss directly, but another condition is
needed. Keep the fixed comparison storage and native form row, and suppose
the phase row is exactly homogeneous of degree one in signed form:

\[
F(ax,\theta;\nu)=aF(x,\theta;\nu),\qquad a\in\mathbb R,
\]

with coefficients, phases and capacities held fixed. This is a condition on
different preparations of the same model, not a conversion of form units.
For an arbitrary proposed phase law write the signed exchange residual

\[
\mathcal R(x,\theta;\nu)=wq^TNg-\beta g^THF,
\qquad \dot E=-L+\mathcal R.
\]

Under this form condition, `R(a*x)=a*R(x)` while `L(a*x)=a^2*L(x)`.
Requiring only total passivity for every signed amplitude therefore gives
`a*R-a^2*L<=0`. Both signs and arbitrarily small amplitudes force `R=0`.
The uniform-locality theorem then leaves only a common angular rate, and
same-model capacity homogeneity removes it. The resulting law is `F_rel`.

Exact degree-one form response is an additional constitutive premise, not a
consequence of the nodal product or of mere oddness under form reflection.
The supplied `rho*Ng` term violates it away from zero phase pressure. The
storage remained fixed throughout this comparison; passivity has not replaced
the loss hypothesis in section 11.3's storage-classification theorem. Higher
order form dependence and other passive completions require their own test,
rather than being excluded by calling the reference canonical.

The [shared admission controls](../../tests/physics/test_relational_exchange_admission.py)
reuse the reference phase geometry and verify the added work, inactive rows,
unit/replica conversion, exact P2 jet, consensus spectrum and signed-amplitude
argument. Detached production field/tangent readings retain their represented
defects separately from these ideal identities. No comparison coefficient is
added to the engine or SDK, and no frozen response is regenerated.

### 11.5 Nonlinear signed form response survives passivity and local recovery

<a id="relational-nonlinear-passive-completion"></a>

The degree-one form response in section 11.4 is sufficient for selection,
but the other retained properties do not force it. Keep the **fixed comparison
storage** `E=E_D+beta*V_phi`, native form pressure, `e,w,beta>0`, held `N>=0`,
and the full regular phase domain on supplied connected simple unit support.
Declare one constant `0<eta<2` and the complete comparison law

\[
\dot x=N(-eD^{-1}q+wg),\qquad F_\eta=F_{\rm rel}+Z,
\]

\[
\boxed{Z_i=\frac{\eta e}{\pi}\nu_i h(s_i)g_i^2,\qquad
s_i=\frac{q_i}{d_i\sqrt\beta},\qquad
h(s)=\frac{s^3}{1+s^2}.}
\]

This is an explicit countermodel, not a derived coefficient or an engine
default. The interval for `eta` is a sufficient declared bound; no optimal
range or search over alternatives is claimed. The denominator is positive
for every finite signed form, so the row is smooth wherever the native
phase geometry is regular.

**Information, parity and units.** The correction uses own capacity and
primitive incident form/phase only. It is capacity-linear, vanishes at zero
capacity, and preserves relabeling, common form shifts and common phase
rotations. Since `h` is odd and `g^2` is even under phase reflection, both
the complete phase row and its correction obey signed-form oddness and
simultaneous `(x,theta)->(-x,-theta)` reversal. At uniform form the correction
and reference phase row vanish. However `h(a*s)` is not generally `a*h(s)`:
oddness and degree-one form response remain distinct requirements.

The coordinate `s_i` is dimensionless. Under section 4's transformations
`x_new=a*x`, `beta_new=a^2*beta`, `N_new=N/b`, it stays unchanged and
`Z_new=Z/b`, with `eta` unchanged. If the pressure weights are renormalized,
`e_new=e/k` and `N_new=k*N/b` compensate in the prefactor as well. Thus no
form or clock conversion is mistaken for a change of the nonlinear response.
On the supplied synchronized complete-replica construction,
`q_fine=m*q`, `d_fine=m*d` and `g_fine=g`; consequently `s` and the phase row
descend unchanged. This prepared compatibility is not closure of an arbitrary
coarse observation or a mechanism of block formation.

**A global pointwise work bound on the regular domain.** Write the signed
extra exchange work as

\[
\mathcal R=-\beta\sum_i g_iH_iZ_i
 =-\frac{\eta e\beta}{\pi}\sum_i\nu_iH_i h(s_i)g_i^3,
\qquad \dot E=-L+\mathcal R.
\]

The native geometry and elementary scalar inequality give

\[
0<H_i\le\pi d_i,\qquad |g_i|<1,\qquad
|h(s)|\le\frac{s^2}{2}.
\]

The last inequality follows from `2*|s|<=1+s^2`. Since
`L=e*beta*sum_i nu_i*d_i*s_i^2`, these independent bounds imply

\[
\boxed{|\mathcal R|\le\frac\eta2L,\qquad
\dot E\le-\left(1-\frac\eta2\right)L\le0.}
\]

The residual can have either sign. It must not be called a nonnegative added
loss: the alternative can dissipate less storage than the reference at one
preparation and more at another, while retaining total passivity. The bound
holds whenever the ideal state is regular; by itself it is not a claim of
global domain invariance or a discrete-step storage guarantee. No loss is
made available to fund a support event without its own reservoir law.

**Equilibria and the retained local mechanism.** For every held nonnegative
capacity vector, the equilibrium conditions remain exactly

\[
Nq=0,\qquad Ng=0.
\]

Indeed, zero velocity implies zero storage derivative, and the strict factor
`1-eta/2>0` forces `L=0`, hence `Nq=0`. The form row then forces `Ng=0`.
Conversely these conditions annul the reference and added rows. Inactive
coordinates retain their frozen-value obstructions; this statement does not
infer global consensus or positive-capacity recovery when some capacities
are zero.

At uniform form and a regular phase equilibrium, `q=0` and `g=0`. For small
joint form/phase perturbations `(u,v)`,

\[
Z=O(\|u\|^3\|v\|^2)=O(\|(u,v)\|^5).
\]

Consequently the reference Jacobian is unchanged. This is a statement about
the vector field's expansion in **state amplitude**; it does not assert that
the first four temporal derivatives agree at a finite perturbed preparation.
At a strictly acute phase equilibrium and positive held capacity, the existing
Hurwitz result therefore gives local exponential recovery for the comparison
as well. Neither the equilibria, signed symmetries nor that linear response
select the complete nonlinear phase law.

**An exact finite-amplitude discriminator.** Fix P2 with
`eta=1`, `e=w=1/2`, `beta=1`, capacities `(1,2)`, forms `(1/2,-1/2)` and
phases `(pi/3,0)`. The state is strictly acute. At this same preparation,

\[
q=(1,-1),\quad g=(-1/3,1/3),\quad H_0=H_1=3\sqrt3/2,
\quad \dot x=(-2/3,4/3),
\]

\[
Z=\left(\frac1{36\pi},-\frac1{18\pi}\right),\qquad
\mathcal R=\frac{\sqrt3}{24\pi}>0,\qquad L=\frac32.
\]

Relative to the reference, the predictions are

\[
\Delta(\dot\theta_0-\dot\theta_1)=\frac1{12\pi},\qquad
\Delta\ddot x=wN J_gZ
 =\left(-\frac1{24\pi^2},\frac1{12\pi^2}\right).
\]

Reflecting the form while holding phase fixed reverses `Z` and `R`, while
leaving the storage and `L` unchanged. Both preparations remain passive.
These are analytic initial-rate distinctions, not sampled trajectories or
physical evidence. The full form-mean equation remains
`mean(xdot)=mean(N*(-eD^-1q+w*g))`: the two laws agree on its initial value,
but their later phase responses change it. In this preparation the difference
of mean form acceleration is `1/(48*pi^2)`. The mean of `Z` is
`-1/(72*pi)` and its relative component is nonzero, so the correction is not
a removable common angular clock.

**The same local geometric basin, with a new proof.** A stronger local
statement can be recovered without transferring a stored trajectory. Use
the [local recovery owner's](#relational-local-recovery) strictly acute
critical phase `theta_*`, its quotient coordinates `z=(u,v)`, and positive
held capacities. Choose

\[
m=\min_{\{i,j\}\in E}(\pi/2-|\delta_{*,ij}|),\qquad
0<r<m/\sqrt2,
\]

\[
c_r=\min_{\{i,j\}\in E}\cos(|\delta_{*,ij}|+\sqrt2r)>0,
\qquad k_r=\frac{\lambda_2(B)}2\min(1,\beta c_r).
\]

The unchanged storage and phase Hessian give, inside `||z||<=r`,

\[
E_{\rm rel}=E_D+\beta[V_\phi(\theta)-V_\phi(\theta_*)]
\ge k_r\|z\|^2.
\]

For `||z(0)||<r` and `E_rel(0)<k_r*r^2`, the newly proved inequality
`Edot_rel<=-(1-eta/2)*L<=0` prevents a first exit from that same ball.
The quotient stays in a compact interior sublevel; the phase metric is bounded
away from zero and the smooth law continues. Thus the **same analytically
specified continuous energy sublevel** is a sufficient domain for this
comparison, by its own argument.

Within it, the largest invariant subset of `L=0` has `q=0`. There `Z=0` and
`F_rel=0`. Keeping `q=0` requires `Ng` to be common across the connected graph;
`sum_i H_i*g_i=0` and positive `H,N` force that common value to vanish. Strict
convexity in the acute quotient neighborhood then selects the reference phase
shape. LaSalle's argument gives convergence, and the unchanged Hurwitz
Jacobian gives exponential convergence near the limit. Common-offset
velocities are smooth functions of the quotient state and vanish there, so
their time integrals converge and the offsets approach finite limits.

This result does not retain a previous quantitative decay constant, validated
trajectory tube, arrival time or proof that a remote formation preparation
enters the basin. Those concern the changed vector field and require their
own evidence. Zero capacities, nonacute targets, events and changing support
are outside this local capture statement.

**Minimal premise ledger and the next scope.** The
[joint-cost theorem](#joint-storage-locality-classification) selects storage
and phase response using its prescribed exact loss and edge-cost class.
With storage fixed, [signed degree-one form response and total passivity](#relational-passive-loss-completion)
are another sufficient route to exact exchange and the reference row. The
present nonlinear alternative meets the listed parity, locality, capacity,
equilibrium and local-recovery requirements while violating that degree-one
condition. Those properties therefore do not derive it. The fixed storage
used for the comparison is not circularly promoted to a theorem about all
passive models, and the supplied `eta` has not become a selected mechanism.

The [shared exact exchange controls](../../tests/physics/test_relational_exchange_admission.py)
check the bound, scaling, equilibrium conditions and finite-amplitude witness.
This closes the declared necessity check. The next formation/interaction
question is whether the full-state two-C5/two-bridge protected-sector capture
argument extends to this admitted comparison. That is a separate gate: the
local-ball proof above neither establishes its larger sector barrier and
capture obligations nor repeats the original formation computation.

### 11.6 Global closure obstruction and a normalized-sine comparison

<a id="global-closure-pressure-comparison"></a>

The [finite-time boundary audit](#regular-domain-continuation-and-boundary-access)
requires distinguishing a limited-domain law from a globally defined one.
The [pressure owner](PRESSURE_CONSTITUTIVE_SCOPE.md#43-phase-domain-orientation-and-a-discriminating-structural-response)
already proves that principal-argument pressure has no continuous extension
through either an antipodal branch or a zero neighbor resultant. With
`w*nu_i>0` the form row inherits that discontinuity. Changing only phase
evolution, storage or locality cannot repair it while leaving that form row
unchanged. The [reflected boundary](RELATIONAL_PATTERN_COMPOSITION.md#reflected-boundary-exit)
additionally has path-dependent phase-rate limits from positive resultants.
A regular invertible chart change removes neither obstruction.

#### The incompatibility is stronger than the local-completion theorem

Take P2 with `r=x_0-x_1!=0`, `delta=theta_1-theta_0` in `(-pi,pi)`,
`c=nu_0+nu_1>0` and arbitrary phase velocities `F_0,F_1`. The native form
row and current storage give, before any locality assumption,

\[
E=r^2/2+\beta(1-\cos\delta),\qquad
\dot E=c[-er^2+wr\delta/\pi]+\beta\sin\delta(F_1-F_0).
\]

Preserving the prescribed exact loss forces

\[
F_0-F_1=\frac{wcr\delta}{\beta\pi\sin\delta}.
\]

This relative rate diverges as `delta->pi` at fixed nonzero form contrast.
It is unaffected by a common phase clock. Dropping capacity separability or
allowing nonlocal phase rows therefore cannot supply a bounded completion
with the same pressure, storage and loss.

Even weakening exact loss to total passivity is insufficient. If `F` were
bounded near this antipodal state, its phase-work term would tend to zero,
leaving `lim Edot=c*(-e*r^2+w*r)`. For `e>0` choose `0<r<w/e`; for `e=0`
any `r>0` suffices. The limit is positive, contradicting passivity.
This argument concerns all-state work identities, not an assertion that
these fixed-form paths are trajectories. Actual finite-time collisions have
their separate proof. The bounded `rho*Ng` and fixed-form nonlinear phase
corrections above retain their regular-domain results but cannot cure this
global obstruction.

| Retained requirement | What it forces or leaves open |
| --- | --- |
| Native principal-argument form pressure on the same circular full state | No globally continuous extension; phase-only changes cannot resolve it |
| That pressure, cosine storage and exact global loss | Unbounded relative phase response near antipodal P2, without needing locality |
| That pressure, cosine storage and only total passivity | Still incompatible with bounded phase completion on all finite form states |
| Restricted invariant preparations | Remain legitimate limited-domain models; the sufficient storage theorem and protected basins apply |
| Changed pressure, enlarged state, event or generalized solution convention | Separate model obligations; none is supplied by assigning a numerical value at zero |

#### Reuse an existing phase reading with an explicit changed premise

The repository already distinguishes `J_phi/pi` from argument pressure.
Write `S_i=sum_j sin(theta_j-theta_i)`, `q=Bx` and `D=diag(d_i)` on
fixed connected simple unit support. The following **comparison law** uses
the same signed form, circular phase, held nonnegative capacity and clock:

\[
s_i=\frac{S_i}{\pi d_i},\qquad
\boxed{\dot x=N(-eD^{-1}q+ws),\quad
       \dot\theta=\frac{w}{\beta\pi}ND^{-1}q.}
\]

Its phase-to-form pressure is `s=J_phi/pi` and its response metric is
`H_s=pi*D`. This replaces both native phase-pressure response and its
matching exchange row. It is not the native law with a different domain
setting, and not the separately supplied Kuramoto/U3 sine phase clock:
here sine enters form pressure, while form contrast drives phase.
Existing current telemetry does not select its use as a dynamical law.

On the native regular domain,

\[
s_i=\frac{|z_i|}{d_i}\operatorname{sinc}(\operatorname{Arg}z_i)\,g_i.
\]

Thus the existing geometric relation directly relates the two responses.
The comparison retains cancellation strength as well as direction, and has
no division by a resultant or its argument. It remains defined when phases
cancel. No new primitive variable, cutoff, fitted gain or boundary event is
introduced. Choosing this pressure is nevertheless an additional premise.

#### Complete balance, continuation and inherited geometry

With the unchanged storage `E=E_D+beta*V_phi`, `grad V_phi=-S` gives

\[
\dot E_D=-e q^TND^{-1}q+\frac w\pi q^TND^{-1}S,\qquad
\beta\dot V_\phi=-\frac w\pi S^TND^{-1}q.
\]

The exchange cancels exactly, leaving the same loss `-e q^TND^{-1}q`.
On any fixed finite graph the complete vector field is globally Lipschitz
on the form/phase lift: its form dependence is linear with constant
coefficients, and sine has bounded derivative. Hence every finite initial
state has a unique global solution, descending to circular phases.
This mathematical theorem concerns the comparison, not the native executor.

For strictly positive held capacities, reciprocity supplies two identities:

\[
\frac{d}{dt}\sum_i\frac{d_i}{\nu_i}x_i=0,\qquad
\frac{d}{dt}\sum_i\frac{d_i}{\nu_i}\theta_i=0.
\]

The second uses a chosen continuous phase lift; it is not in general a
single-valued scalar observation on the phase torus. At zero capacity these
weighted expressions are unavailable, although the smooth law and work
balance remain valid. The native law generally conserves neither mean.

With all capacities positive, equilibria are exactly `q=0,S=0`. All native
regular equilibria therefore survive, but the complete set grows: zero and
negative-real resultants with `S=0` are now allowed. The seven-equilibrium
classification must not be transferred as exhaustive.
At each equilibrium the existing
[full-network stiffness proof](#regular-equilibrium-stiffness) applies with
`H=pi*D`: positive quotient cosine Hessian gives local exponential recovery
for `e,w,beta>0`, and negative stiffness gives growing real modes.
Consensus has exactly the native linearization. Away from consensus, equal
geometry and stability signs do not give equal rates, memory or basins.

For `e>0` and positive capacities, storage and conserved weighted form mean
give precompactness on form times the phase torus. On the largest invariant
subset with zero loss, `q=0` and
`BND^-1 S=0`, so `ND^-1 S=c*1`. Reciprocity `sum_i S_i=0` forces `c=0`.
LaSalle therefore gives approach to the equilibrium **set**. This does not
prove convergence to consensus, to one equilibrium, or to a physical entity.

#### Conditional selection and remaining freedom

There is a more structural selection route than assuming a constant metric.
Keep cosine storage, the same diffusion channel and exact loss on the full
phase torus for every finite connected simple unit graph and every
nonnegative capacity vector. Require zero-capacity phase freezing, own-capacity
separability and a finite continuous phase law. The **additional pressure
premise** is normalized pairwise superposition,

\[
g_i(\theta)=d_i^{-1}\sum_{j\sim i}f(\theta_j-\theta_i),
\]

with one continuous, periodic, odd kernel `f` across supports. On a star with
only the root active and arbitrary `q_i!=0`, exact loss gives
`beta*S_i*F_i=w*nu_i*q_i*g_i`. Thus every finite family with
`sum_j sin(delta_j)=0` must also have `sum_j f(delta_j)=0`.
All phases, including cancellations and antipodal leaves, are admitted by
the proposed global completion; no regular-domain padding is required.

Reuse the [balanced-star algebra](../TNFR_VARIATIONAL_PRINCIPLE.md#native-phase-storage-classification)
with a different object being classified. The two gaps
`delta,-arcsin(sin(delta))` give
`f(delta)=f(arcsin(sin(delta)))`. Put `h(s)=f(arcsin(s))`. Three gaps with
sines `a,b,-(a+b)` give `h(a+b)=h(a)+h(b)` whenever all arguments lie in
`[-1,1]`. Continuity implies `h(s)=k*s` and therefore `f(delta)=k*sin(delta)`.
Matching the native consensus tangent fixes `k=1/pi`.
Capacity isolation now forces
`F_i=w*nu_i*q_i/(beta*pi*d_i)` whenever `S_i!=0`; own-capacity separability
transfers this row to general capacities. Nonzero `S_i` states are dense
by perturbing a neighbor phase, including near zero resultants, so continuity
extends the row everywhere. Varying only the root phase would not justify
that density at a zero neighbor resultant.

This derives the metric and phase independence instead of assuming them.
It does not derive pairwise superposition, which the native collective Arg
source does not obey. A separate sufficient selection premise is to require
the phase row itself to be phase-independent while leaving the phase-only
pressure initially unrestricted: the same isolated-capacity identity and
consensus tangent then also force the displayed comparison.

Neither selection premise follows from smoothness, storage or circular symmetry.
For example, the comparison family

\[
m_i(\theta)=\frac{1+\epsilon(S_i/d_i)^2}{\pi d_i},\quad
g_i=m_iS_i,\quad
F_i=(w/\beta)\nu_i m_i q_i,\qquad \epsilon\ge0
\]

retains smoothness, positive mobility, exact loss, the same equilibria and
the same consensus Jacobian. It supplies a counterexample to unique selection,
not another production setting. The
[frozen pairing comparison](../TNFR_SCALE_GEOMETRY_AND_BRIDGE.md#sine-pairing-constitutive-scope)
reuses its zero-loss members to separate a robust local grouping direction
from different relative response rates. The family generally violates the
common pairwise-superposition premise. The original storage
classification assumed native pressure; changing that premise means cosine
storage is retained here explicitly, not rederived by that old theorem.

#### An exact discriminator before any trajectory

On a three-leaf star, take center phase zero, leaf phases `(0,0,pi/2)`,
uniform form and positive held capacities. All native resultants are regular.
Put `W=sum_i d_i/nu_i` and let `M` be the degree/inverse-capacity weighted
form mean. At this same initial state,

\[
\dot M_{\rm native}
=\frac wW\left[\frac{3\arctan(1/2)}{\pi}-\frac12\right]<0,\qquad
\dot M_{\rm sine}=0.
\]

The sign follows from `atan(1/2)<1/2<pi/6`. Both initial phase rates are
zero, and both laws dissipate zero storage initially; the mean response
still distinguishes them. This is an exact prospective initial-rate
comparison with identical preparation, coefficients and clock, not a fitted
response or physical validation. The older same-mean/different-spread
pressure test remains useful; no frozen acquisition is rerun.

There is also a purely spectral distinction at the same maintained C5
geometry. For a uniform acute cycle twist with `c=cos(kappa)>0`, common
capacity `nu` and nonzero combinatorial Laplacian eigenvalue `ell`, write
`z=2*s/(nu*ell)` for the scaled temporal exponent. The two characteristic
polynomials are

\[
z^2+ez+\frac{w^2}{\beta\pi^2c}=0\quad\text{(native)},\qquad
z^2+ez+\frac{w^2c}{\beta\pi^2}=0\quad\text{(comparison)}.
\]

For winding-one C5, `e=w=1/2,beta=1` and `c=cos(2*pi/5)<1/3`.
The native discriminant `1/4-1/(pi^2*c)` is negative, whereas the comparison
discriminant `1/4-c/pi^2` is positive. Both recover locally, but one has
damped oscillatory linear modes and the other distinct negative real modes.
Local pulse character therefore depends on the constitutive response, even
when the stable geometry and storage agree. This is a tangent theorem,
not a computed nonlinear response or a transfer of earlier memory records.

[`bound_relational_sine_exchange`](../../src/tnfr/physics/relational_sine_comparison.py)
is a detached static comparison with explicit law identity and exact
represented-state trigonometric bounds. It reuses shared graph/scalar
admission and report projection, while preserving native phase-domain
rejection. Its computed interval work residuals are distinct from the
analytic cancellation identity. It installs no dynamics, boundary extension
or automatic model selector. Frozen transit, capture, memory and formation
responses remain evidence of their original law.

### 11.7 Common exchange geometry, composition and a controlled common regime

<a id="common-exchange-geometry-and-composition"></a>

The two complete laws above belong to one mathematical family. This is an
identity between declared models, not a derivation of a uniquely selected
microscopic law. Keep the same support, clock, held capacities, coefficients
and storage. Write `M=diag(m_i)` with finite positive entries on its stated
domain. Then

\[
\dot x=N(-eD^{-1}q+wMS),\qquad
\dot\theta=(w/\beta)NMq
\]

gives `Edot=-e*q^T*N*D^-1*q` for every such mobility. In fact
`grad_x E=q` and `grad_theta E=-beta*S`, so the two exchange work terms
cancel by symmetry of `NM`. No derivative of `M` enters this first storage
derivative, even if `M` depends on the state. Existence, regularity, symmetries
and higher derivatives still require their own hypotheses. Equal loss
functions do not imply equal storage histories along different trajectories.

The native choice is `m_i=1/H_i`, while the normalized-sine comparison uses
`m_i=1/(pi*d_i)`. The common skew work structure therefore leaves precisely
the constitutive mobility being investigated. It does not by itself give a
Poisson bracket: the earlier [Jacobi audit](../TNFR_VARIATIONAL_PRINCIPLE.md#1319-structural-closure-tests-exchange-jacobi-and-the-remaining-potential)
distinguishes that extra requirement from work cancellation.

#### Geometric attenuation is not generally a change of clock

On the native regular domain put `z_i=R_i*exp(i*alpha_i)`. The exact ratio is

\[
a_i=\frac{m_i^{\rm sine}}{m_i^{\rm native}}
 =\frac{R_i}{d_i}\operatorname{sinc}\alpha_i
 =\frac{H_i}{\pi d_i},\qquad 0<a_i\le1.
\]

Here `R_i<=d_i` and `0<sinc(alpha_i)<=1`. Equality requires all neighbors
to have the center's phase modulo a turn. The sine law scales both nodal
exchange terms by `a_i` and leaves form diffusion unchanged. Different
nodes generally have different attenuations.

Even P2 with positive diffusion rules out a common clock interpretation.
Set `r=x_0-x_1`, `delta=theta_1-theta_0` in `(-pi,pi)` and
`c=nu_0+nu_1>0`. In these two relative coordinates the native and sine
velocity vectors are

\[
F_n=\left(c[-er+w\delta/\pi],
 -\frac{cw r\delta}{\beta\pi\sin\delta}\right),\qquad
F_s=\left(c[-er+w\sin\delta/\pi],
 -\frac{cw r}{\beta\pi}\right).
\]

Thus

\[
\det(F_n,F_s)=\frac{c^2ewr^2}{\beta\pi}
 \left(1-\frac{\delta}{\sin\delta}\right)\ne0
\]

when `e>0,r!=0,0<|delta|<pi`. A regular common clock change multiplies the
whole vector field by one positive scalar, so it cannot identify these
fields, even after removing common offsets. For `e=0` on P2 only,
`F_s=sinc(delta)*F_n`: there is a restricted orbit equivalence. It is not
general on larger graphs; the preceding star's weighted-mean discriminator
also applies at `e=0` and forbids a positive common multiplier there.

#### A controlled near-alignment regime

Suppose all wrapped incident gaps satisfy `|delta_ij|<=epsilon<pi/2`.
Then `C_i>=d_i*cos(epsilon)` and `|alpha_i|<=epsilon`. Since
`H_i/pi=C_i*tan(alpha_i)/alpha_i>=C_i` with the removable value at zero,

\[
\cos\epsilon\le a_i\le1,\qquad
|g_i-s_i|\le\frac{(1-\cos\epsilon)\epsilon}{\pi},
\]
\[
|\dot\theta_i^{\,n}-\dot\theta_i^{\,s}|
 \le\frac{w\nu_i|q_i|}{\beta\pi d_i}(\sec\epsilon-1),\qquad
|\dot x_i^{\,n}-\dot x_i^{\,s}|
 \le\frac{w\nu_i}{\pi}(1-\cos\epsilon)\epsilon.
\]

These are instantaneous bounds at a shared state, not trajectory enclosures.
The phase discrepancy is `O(epsilon^2*|q_i|)`; the complete discrepancy
is cubic only when form contrasts are also `O(epsilon)`. A future error
estimate must additionally bound the field Lipschitz constant and preserve
the neighborhood throughout the comparison horizon. The common small-signal
regime is derived; the ratio does not supply an autonomous switching rule.

#### Composition exposes an independent premise

At a fixed root state let `P(A)` be the phase source of a nonempty finite
multiset of incident gaps `A`; equal entries mean different neighbors with
the same phase, not parallel edges. **Independent normalized composition**
would require, for every partition into disjoint incident groups,

\[
(|A|+|B|)P(A\sqcup B)=|A|P(A)+|B|P(B).
\]

Induction over singleton groups makes this condition equivalent to
`P(A)=sum_{delta in A} f(delta)/|A|` with `f(delta)=P({delta})`.
It is exactly the added superposition premise used in the preceding
classification, rather than a consequence of that classification.

Both candidates pass equal replication: duplicating every incident
coordinate multiplies `d,S,H,q` by the same factor and preserves their
complete nodal rates. That test is strictly weaker than independent
composition. For example, take `A=(0,0)`, `B=(t)` with `0<t<pi/2`.
Native source composition would require
`Arg(2+exp(i*t))=t/3`. Instead

\[
\frac{d}{dt}\left[\operatorname{Arg}(2+e^{it})-\frac t3\right]
 =\frac{2(\cos t-1)}{3(5+4\cos t)}<0.
\]

The merger defect is nonzero although all groups are regular. Sine sources
obey the merger identity exactly. The smooth collective counterfamily in
section 11.6 also preserves replication while generally violating merger.
Neither nodal locality nor replication establishes independent composition.

The [stationary mediator reduction](RELATIONAL_PATTERN_MEMORY.md#sine-mediator-common-geometry)
answers the associated scale question constructively: even a primitive
pairwise sine law induces collective visible interaction after eliminating
one node. It shares the native conditional minimum and inherited storage,
but transmits a different pressure. An emergent `Arg` coordinate is therefore
not a derivation of native `Arg` pressure. Finite hidden capacity retains a
tracking defect and memory; its controlled fast limit has explicit premises.

The [composition controls](../../tests/physics/test_relational_pressure_composition.py)
exercise merger, replication, the local discrepancy bounds and clock
non-equivalence using existing field owners. The
[detached mediation report](../../src/tnfr/physics/relational_sine_mediation.py)
integrates the justified reduction without selecting a new primitive law.
Together these results identify a common exchange geometry and a common
local regime, while leaving the deeper mobility-selection hypothesis open.

### 11.8 Closed form balance can select primitive pressure composition

<a id="closed-form-balance-and-source-selection"></a>

The composition premise has an equivalent whole-graph interpretation within
an explicit information class. This supplies another conditional selection
route; neither conservation nor that information class follows from the
nodal identity. Here a closed graph means that the entire fixed support is
included, with no external source or event. Degree-one vertices are allowed.

Keep finite connected simple unit support with at least two nodes, held
positive capacities, the support Laplacian `B=D-A` and `q=Bx` from section 1,
and the form row

\[
\dot x=N(-eD^{-1}Bx+wg),\qquad e\ge0,\quad w>0.
\]

Require one common phase-source family across supports,
`g_i=P_{d_i}(A_i)`, where `A_i` is the multiset of circular incident gaps
`theta_j-theta_i`. The source consumes only these gaps and their count. It
does not consume form, capacity, neighbor degrees, connections between
neighbors, graph identity, or hidden state. Equal gaps can represent distinct
neighbors. This is stronger than unspecified one-hop locality: replacing
the root's environment by a star must preserve its source. No phase row or
storage identity is needed for the next equivalence.

#### Diffusion fixes the weight of a possible linear form invariant

For `e>0`, suppose a nonzero state-independent vector `a`, allowed to depend
on the held graph and capacities, makes `a^T*x` invariant at every form and
phase state. Varying form at fixed phase removes the phase-only source and
gives

\[
a^TND^{-1}B=0,\qquad BD^{-1}Na=0.
\]

Connectedness gives `ker(B)=span{1}`, hence
`a_i=c*d_i/nu_i` for one nonzero constant `c`. Thus the only possible
nontrivial linear form invariant has, up to scale, the weights

\[
\mathcal Q=\sum_i\frac{d_i}{\nu_i}x_i,\qquad
W=\sum_i\frac{d_i}{\nu_i},\qquad M=\mathcal Q/W.
\]

This identifies the weight if such an invariant is required; it does not
establish its existence. For `e=0` this uniqueness argument is unavailable.
For the displayed weight and any `e>=0`, the exact accounting identity is

\[
\dot{\mathcal Q}=w\sum_i d_i g_i.
\]

The positive-capacity premise matters: these inverse-capacity quantities
are unavailable at a zero-capacity node.

#### Whole-graph balance is equivalent to an odd pairwise source

In the gap-only source class, require `sum_i d_i*g_i=0` for every phase
state on every finite connected support. It suffices to require this on
every star. Put `f(delta)=P_1({delta})`. On the single-edge graph the two
root gaps are `delta` and `-delta`, so balance gives
`f(delta)+f(-delta)=0`. On a star with root phase zero and arbitrary leaf
phases `delta_1,...,delta_d`, the root has source `P_d(A)` and each leaf
has source `f(-delta_j)`. Therefore

\[
0=dP_d(A)+\sum_{j=1}^d f(-\delta_j),\qquad
\boxed{P_d(A)=\frac1d\sum_{j=1}^d f(\delta_j),\quad
       f(-\delta)=-f(\delta).}
\]

Conversely, summing this formula over any reciprocal graph cancels the
two orientations of each edge. Whole-graph balance is therefore equivalent
to normalized pairwise superposition with one odd circular kernel, and to
the preceding independent normalized-composition identity together with
singleton oddness. No continuity or differentiability is used. Requiring
the balance at one positive capacity assignment per star is enough because
the source is capacity-independent and the capacities cancel from the
accounting identity.

The support and information quantifiers cannot be discarded. Testing only
regular graphs permits `g_i=a_{d_i}*sum_j sin(delta_ij)/d_i` with arbitrary
degree-dependent gains. Consuming neighbor degrees instead permits

\[
g_i=\frac1{d_i}\sum_{j\sim i}(d_i+d_j)\sin\delta_{ij},
\]

which conserves the same weighted form on every graph by edge cancellation,
but has no common singleton kernel across supports. It lies outside the
stated gap-only information class. Ordinary locality, relabeling covariance
or equal replication does not supply the missing restriction.

#### Cosine exchange selects sine, with a separate phase-row premise

Combine this conservation premise with the global comparison domain and
storage assumptions of [section 11.6](#global-closure-pressure-comparison):
continuous circular pressure on the full phase torus, the retained storage
`E_D+beta*V_phi`, its exact loss `-e*q^T*N*D^-1*q`, and finite phase
velocities that freeze at zero own capacity. The complete law must admit
all nonnegative held capacity vectors; weighted form itself is used only
on the positive-capacity part of that domain.

Activate only a star's root capacity and choose `q_root!=0`. All other
phase rows freeze, so exact loss leaves

\[
\beta S_{\rm root}F_{\rm root}
 =w\nu_{\rm root}q_{\rm root}P_d(A).
\]

Whenever `sum_j sin(delta_j)=0`, finiteness forces
`sum_j f(delta_j)=0`. The balanced-star argument in section 11.6 then gives
`f(delta)=k*sin(delta)`; matching the native consensus tangent fixes
`k=1/pi`. Thus conservation plus the stated global exchange premises
selects the normalized-sine **pressure** without independently assuming
pairwise superposition. This reuses the existing classification rather
than deriving cosine storage or conservation from it.

Own-capacity separability is an additional requirement for selecting the
complete phase row. With that premise and continuity of the phase law,
capacity isolation at each node first forces the row where `S_i!=0`,
transfers it to arbitrary capacity vectors, and extends it by continuity
to `S_i=0`:

\[
g_i=\frac{S_i}{\pi d_i},\qquad
F_i=\frac{w\nu_iq_i}{\beta\pi d_i}.
\]

Without the extra phase-row premise, the pressure selection does not remove
all globally work-null phase velocities. The required conservation law,
cosine storage, prescribed loss, capacity behavior, full phase domain and
consensus normalization remain independent constitutive assumptions.

#### The combined premises also cover induced-neighborhood pressure

The sine-pressure conclusion survives a larger information class when both
conservation and exact cosine exchange are imposed. Allow pressure to consume
the phases and topology of the induced rooted ball `G[{i} union N(i)]`,
including edges between neighbors. Require one rule invariant under rooted
isomorphism and common phase rotation across all supports, independent of
form and capacity. External neighbor degrees, remote state and hidden
support-specific coefficients remain excluded. This does not extend the
conservation-alone equivalence to arbitrary local sources.

First isolate one active capacity in the global storage identity. For every
node, `S_i=0` forces `g_i=0`: choose `q_i!=0`, use finiteness of its phase
row and freezing of all other phase rows. This implication holds at every
capacity assignment because pressure is capacity-independent. On P2,
whole-graph balance again makes the common singleton kernel `f` odd.

Adapt the [induced-neighborhood completion](#primitive-locality-exchange-selection).
Retain an arbitrary root ball with all its internal edges and phases. For
each root neighbor `j` and every retained incidence `j-k`, attach one private
leaf to `j` with phase `2*theta_j-theta_k`. No new leaf touches the root, so
its entire source information is unchanged. Every retained neighbor now has
`S_j=0`, and hence `g_j=0`, because each old sine contribution has its
opposite at the reflected leaf. Unlike the native regular-domain completion,
no aligned padding is needed: the full phase torus admits zero or negative
resultants.

Assign strictly positive capacities throughout this completed graph before
using whole-graph form balance. A reflected leaf at `j-k` contributes
`f(theta_k-theta_j)`. Leaves associated with the two incidences of an edge
between root neighbors cancel by oddness. The remaining leaves correspond
to root edges and contribute `-sum_{j~i}f(theta_j-theta_i)`. Therefore

\[
d_i g_i=\sum_{j\sim i}f(\theta_j-\theta_i).
\]

The original root inherits this identity by uniform rooted locality. The
preceding balanced-star classification now selects normalized sine. No
locality or capacity separability of the phase row was used to select this
pressure; uniqueness of its complete phase row retains the earlier extra
premise. The proof never evaluates inverse-capacity form weights at zero
capacity. These completion graphs are mathematical comparison states, not
support events. The [locality controls](../../tests/physics/test_relational_locality_selection.py)
check the retained root ball and reflected-leaf cancellation separately
from the existing native-domain completion.

#### Branching tests information that paths and cycles cannot determine

Even conservation, composition, smoothness, the consensus tangent and exact
cosine-storage loss on every path and cycle do not select sine. For
`epsilon>0`, take the common odd kernel

\[
f_\epsilon(\delta)=\frac{\sin\delta+\epsilon\sin^3\delta}{\pi}.
\]

On graphs of maximum degree two, let `s_j=sin(delta_j)` at a root and use
the finite positive mobility

\[
m_1=\frac{1+\epsilon s_1^2}{\pi},\qquad
m_2=\frac{1+\epsilon(s_1^2-s_1s_2+s_2^2)}{2\pi}.
\]

The identity `s_1^3+s_2^3=(s_1+s_2)(s_1^2-s_1*s_2+s_2^2)` gives
`P_d=m_d*S`. Consequently `F_i=(w/beta)*nu_i*m_i*q_i` supplies a smooth
full-state exchange completion with the prescribed exact loss, zero-capacity
freezing and own-capacity separability on this restricted support class.
Its tangent is the normalized-sine tangent, but its nonlinear pressure
differs.

A degree-three star rejects that same kernel. Choose the acute leaf gaps
`(arcsin(1/4),arcsin(1/4),-pi/6)`. Their sine sum is zero while their sine
cubes sum to `-3/32`, giving

\[
P_3=-\frac{\epsilon}{32\pi}\ne0.
\]

With only the root capacity active and nonzero root form contrast, its
phase work is zero and its source work is nonzero. No finite phase row
with the stated freezing can supply the prescribed loss. The obstruction
occurs strictly inside an acute phase chart, not only at a zero resultant or
an antipodal boundary. Degree-three branching is therefore indispensable
to this countercontrol; further path or cycle trajectories cannot resolve
the same constitutive ambiguity.

<a id="phase-storage-selection-boundary"></a>

The fixed cosine-storage premise is essential to that obstruction. The
same cubic pressure has an all-degree completion if its phase potential is
changed explicitly to

\[
U_\epsilon(\delta)=1-\cos\delta+
\epsilon\left(\frac23-\cos\delta+\frac{\cos^3\delta}{3}\right),
\qquad \epsilon>0.
\]

For `c=cos(delta)` this is
`(1-c)+epsilon*(1-c)^2*(c+2)/3>=0`, with `U_epsilon(0)=0`.
Moreover `U_epsilon'=sin(delta)+epsilon*sin(delta)^3` and
`U_epsilon''(0)=1`. On any admitted finite support define

\[
\widetilde S_i=\sum_{j\sim i}U_\epsilon'(\theta_j-\theta_i),\qquad
g_i=\frac{\widetilde S_i}{\pi d_i},\qquad
F_i=\frac{w\nu_iq_i}{\beta\pi d_i},\qquad
E_\epsilon=E_D+\beta\sum_{\{i,j\}}U_\epsilon(\theta_j-\theta_i).
\]

Then `grad_theta E_epsilon=-beta*tilde S`, so the same constant
reciprocal Poisson tensor and form-diffusion block reproduce both rows,
and `dot E_epsilon=-e*q^T*N*D^-1*q`. Odd edge currents preserve weighted
form balance at positive held capacities. The pressure is smooth and
pairwise, and its complete consensus Jacobian equals the normalized-sine
one, although its nonlinear phase potential and pressure differ.

At the degree-three witness the original sine sum vanishes, but the
changed phase gradient does not; the exchange therefore has the missing
phase work. This countermodel shows why conservation, Jacobi, smoothness
and the consensus response still do not select sine without a phase-storage
premise. It changes that premise, rather than evading the fixed-cosine
selection theorem. Cosine-storage capture certificates and the earlier
native-pressure-derived storage classification cannot be transferred to
it without checking their changed hypotheses. It supplies no new default
law, runtime setting or physical storage identification.

#### Existing acute-star observations separate the premises

Reuse the preceding composition control's incident-gap preparation: a star
with root phase zero and leaf phases `(0,0,t)`, `0<t<pi/2`. The balance
control fixes `t=1/2`, `x=(0,0,0,0)`, capacities `(1,2,3,4)` in root-first
order, `e=0`, `w=beta=1`, no Gamma or events, and `epsilon=1` for the
current-squared comparison. The source laws, clock and exact preparation
are declared before this static evaluation; no trajectory is acquired.
Reuse of the earlier gaps is distinct from evaluating these whole-form
observations. No earlier frozen response or producer is regenerated.

More generally, at uniform form and arbitrary positive held capacities,
all three laws have zero phase velocity and zero instantaneous storage
loss at this star. Their weighted-form mean rates nonetheless differ:

\[
\dot M_{\rm native}
 =\frac{w}{\pi W}\left[
 3\arctan\!\left(\frac{\sin t}{2+\cos t}\right)-t\right]<0,
\qquad \dot M_{\rm sine}=0,
\]
\[
\dot M_{\rm current^2}
 =-\frac{8w\epsilon\sin^3t}{9\pi W}<0
 \qquad(\epsilon>0).
\]

The native sign is the already proved merger defect. For the existing
current-squared family, the root contributes
`(1+epsilon*sin(t)^2/9)*sin(t)/pi` to `sum_i d_i*g_i`, and the nonaligned
leaf contributes `-(1+epsilon*sin(t)^2)*sin(t)/pi`. Their difference gives
the displayed expression. These are exact conditional initial-rate
identities; the shared static readers check represented-state observations
and intervals separately. The comparison reuses the existing laws and
preparation rather than installing a new dynamics or claiming physical
selection from a computed response.

The [composition controls](../../tests/physics/test_relational_pressure_composition.py)
exercise these balance and branching boundaries. Whole-graph form balance
concerns the held-support continuous law. Changing degrees or capacities
changes its weights, and hybrid events need their own jump accounting.
The invariant is not identified with physical mass, energy or a substrate
origin. The conservation-only equivalence concerns the gap-only information
class; the induced-neighborhood extension also requires exact cosine
exchange. Neither conclusion selects a universal law or changes the native
runtime and retained evidence.

## 12. A capacity-mediated alternative and a discriminating intervention

<a id="capacity-mediated-exchange-counterexample"></a>

The missing capacity premise is consequential. Reuse the earlier
[power-null construction's method](../TNFR_VARIATIONAL_PRINCIPLE.md#1319-structural-closure-tests-exchange-jacobi-and-the-remaining-potential),
but retain this owner's Dirichlet storage and exact native form row. Write
`a=grad(V_phi)`, `ell=D^-1 Bx` and define the symmetric pair mobility

\[
\mu_{ij}=\begin{cases}
\nu_i\nu_j/(\nu_i+\nu_j),&\nu_i+\nu_j>0,\\
0,&\nu_i=\nu_j=0.
\end{cases}
\]

For adjacent nodes set

\[
S_{ij}=\frac w\beta\frac{\mu_{ij}}{d_i d_j}
       (\ell_i+\ell_j)\sin(\theta_j-\theta_i),\qquad
S_{ii}=0,\qquad Z=Sa.
\]

Nonadjacent entries vanish. The factors other than the sine are symmetric,
so `S^T=-S` and `a^T Z=0` identically. Both `F_rel` and `F_rel+Z` therefore
have the same complete storage loss and unmodified form pressure. In
general they redistribute nonzero nodal phase work while its sum cancels.
This is an explicit **countermodel**, not a selected correction or a new
production phase rule.

This alternative uses only existing form, phase, capacity and support:

- It is equivariant under node relabeling, common form shifts, common phase
  rotations and simultaneous `(x,theta)->(-x,-theta)` reversal.
- `nu_i=0` makes row `S_i` zero. The pair mobility is homogeneous of degree
  one in all capacities, but not additive in independent capacity vectors.
  It is continuous at joint zero and smooth on positive strata; no capacity
  derivative at the joint-zero corner is claimed. Held capacities give a
  smooth field in evolving form and phase throughout the regular chart.
- The section 4 unit/clock conversions preserve this field, including the
  compensating capacity change when channel weights are renormalized.
- Equal complete replicas preserve the correction: `ell` is unchanged,
  `a_fine=m*a`, each pair coefficient becomes `S_ij/m^2`, and each base
  neighbor supplies `m` identical contributions. Disjoint admitted components are
  also independent. Thus these composition checks do not imply capacity
  additivity at a fixed state and support.

**Locality boundary.** On K3 every primitive consumed is within one hop.
On general graphs, reading a neighbor's `ell` and `a` reaches two primitive
neighborhoods. The replica identity does not turn that full fine law into
a globally one-hop implementation. This example disproves uniqueness from
the stated fixed-K3 balance, symmetry and zero-capacity conditions, and
from their general finite-locality counterpart. It does **not** disprove a
stronger theorem demanding one uniform primitive-neighborhood law on all
admitted graphs. [Section 11.1](#primitive-locality-exchange-selection) now
proves that conditional result without assuming closure of unprepared
coarse refinements.

**Exact nonconsensus witness.** On unit K3 take
`x=(1/3,0,-1/3)`, `theta=(0,pi/6,-pi/6)`, `nu=(1,1,1)`. All resultants and
phase displacements lie in the admitted chart. With `k=w/beta`,

\[
Z=\frac{k}{64}
\begin{pmatrix}1+\sqrt3\\-3-\sqrt3\\-3-\sqrt3\end{pmatrix},
\qquad
J_gZ=\frac{k(2+\sqrt3)}{64\pi}
\begin{pmatrix}-2\\1\\1\end{pmatrix}\ne0.
\]

Both laws have identical `x`, pressure and instantaneous `xdot`. Their
phase-source rates differ by `J_gZ`, and their form accelerations by
`w*N*J_gZ`. This is relative evolution, not a removable common rotation.
At phase consensus the correction vanishes, so the frozen section 9
discriminator cannot separate these two members of the relational-storage
family. The new witness addresses the missing premise directly.

Hold that form and phase fixed, keep `nu_0=nu_2=1`, and change only
`nu_1` from `1` to `1/2`. The selected capacity-separable law leaves `F_0`
unchanged. The alternative changes its extra row by

\[
\Delta F_0=\Delta Z_0=-\frac{w(1+\sqrt3)}{192\beta}\ne0.
\]

The same intervention admits a relative-phase observation, independent of a
common phase reference. Put `R=F_0-F_2`. Both unchanged-capacity baseline rows
are invariant under this intervention, whereas the countermodel also has
`Delta Z_2=k*(3+sqrt(3))/192`. Therefore

\[
\Delta R_{\rm separable}=0,\qquad
\Delta R_{\rm mediated}=-\frac{k(2+\sqrt3)}{96},\qquad k=w/\beta>0.
\]

These are ideal differences at the same declared nonconsensus preparation,
not a finite-sample acquisition. Relative phase removes a common rotation;
it does not remove rate units or observation uncertainty. A uniform positive
separation from zero requires a positive lower bound on `k` in that clock,
or an admitted normalized observation cancelling the unknown scale.
Identifying `chi` alone does not supply such a bound. For instance,
`(e,w,beta)=(1/2,1/2,1)` and `(1/3,2/3,4)` both have `chi=1`, but `k=1/2`
and `1/6`, respectively. Other retained coefficient/clock information must
justify any absolute prediction. Both candidates must keep the same admitted
preparation and parameter information through the reserved comparison.

No pressure is reconstructed from a response and no scale is refitted.
This exact constitutive intervention distinguishes local independent
activity from one admissible pair-mediated activity. Independent physical
preparation/observation of such an intervention remains unestablished.

Controls: the
[capacity-identification test](../../tests/physics/test_relational_exchange_admission.py)
solves for unknown nonlinear own-capacity coefficients using the complete
storage chain rule on an irregular P3. The
[selection counterexample tests](../../tests/physics/test_relational_exchange_selection.py)
reuse the shared exact phase geometry and check the full work identity,
zero capacities, nonadditivity, source-response difference, capacity
intervention, transformations and complete replicas. These controls add
no temporal campaign or default engine dynamics.

### A scale-independent reserved capacity test

<a id="normalized-capacity-discriminator"></a>

**K3 relative-rate protocol v1 (2026-10-03).** This section specifies the
conditional analytic prediction and uncertainty budget; the separate
[bounded software response](#capacity-discriminator-sampled-response) tests
that protocol. Compare exactly the two complete laws above: the native form row with phase row
`F_rel`, versus the same form row with `F_rel+Z`. Both hold the unit K3 support
and capacities fixed, use the same `e>=0`, `w>0`, `beta>0` and structural clock,
have no forcing or events, and evolve both form and phase in the regular chart.
The capacity change defines **two matched initial preparations**, not two
successive times on a freely evolving trajectory or an installed capacity law.
Use the exact form/phase witness above, with capacities `(1,1,1)` and
`(1,1/2,1)`, respectively. No nodal readout is replaced by tetrad telemetry.

The prepared geometry gives

\[
Bx=(1,0,-1)^T,\qquad
H=\operatorname{diag}(\pi\sqrt3,2(1+\sqrt3),2(1+\sqrt3)).
\]

Write

\[
\alpha=\frac1{\pi\sqrt3}+\frac1{2(1+\sqrt3)},\qquad
c=\frac{2+\sqrt3}{32},\qquad k=\frac w\beta.
\]

For the relative initial phase rate `R=F_0-F_2`, the exact predictions are

| Candidate | Before preparation | After preparation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Capacity-separable | `k*alpha` | `k*alpha` |
| Named capacity-mediated alternative | `k*(alpha+c)` | `k*(alpha+2*c/3)` |

Normalize each candidate by **its own** before rate:

\[
Q=\frac{R_{after}}{R_{before}}-1,\qquad
Q_{sep}=0,\quad Q_{med}=-d,\quad
d=\frac{c}{3(\alpha+c)}\simeq0.0804184436792551.
\]

Both denominators are positive. An unknown common nonzero observation gain
and positive constant affine-clock scale multiply both rates and cancel,
as does `k`. Relative phase removes a common angular reference. Distinct
gains/clock rates between preparations, additive rate offsets or a changed
preparation do not cancel. A derivative readout must use a consistent local
phase lift; differencing wrapped angles across a branch cut is not admitted.
The coordinates and intervention are prescribed in the same form/capacity
units for both laws; normalization does not authenticate that preparation.

This removes the need to identify `k` or tighten the previous chi estimate
for this discriminator. Coefficient calibration remains a separate result:
its uncertainty is not set to zero, but neither `chi` nor `k` is consumed by
this normalized prediction. The known-source P2 record is therefore not
reused as blind calibration. No response is used to fit either law.

**Prospective error admission.** Choose the common readout orientation so
the ideal before rate `B` is positive. Supply measured rate estimates `m_b`,
`m_a` and independently justified total errors `epsilon_b`, `epsilon_a`.
Freeze the following sufficient signal-resolution gate before acquisition:

\[
L=m_b-\epsilon_b>0,\qquad
0\le\epsilon_b,\epsilon_a\le L/200.
\]

The errors must enclose the difference from the specified ideal rates, not
just sensor noise. Exact preparation is the present mathematical premise;
otherwise form, phase, capacity, support, timing and gain discrepancies need
their own justified propagation into this budget. Since `L<=B`, each error
is at most `B/200`. For the two measured intervals
`I_b=[m_b-epsilon_b,m_b+epsilon_b]` and
`I_a=[m_a-epsilon_a,m_a+epsilon_a]`, each endpoint can deviate by at most
`B/100` from the corresponding true rate: centering on a noisy measurement
and then padding it are two separate contributions.

With `eta=1/100`, monotonicity of the positive-denominator ratio gives the
exact-arithmetic report envelopes

\[
J_{sep}=\left[-\frac{2\eta}{1+\eta},\frac{2\eta}{1-\eta}\right],\qquad
J_{med}=\left[\frac{-d-2\eta}{1+\eta},
                   \frac{-d+2\eta}{1-\eta}\right].
\]

They are disjoint whenever `eta<d/(4-d)`, which this budget satisfies.
Equivalently, every exact quotient corner differs from its ideal `Q` by at
most `2/99`. Reserve a further arithmetic allowance `1/1584` on each side:
the final report must then lie within `Q +/- 1/48`, since
`2/99+1/1584=1/48`. These two enlarged envelopes are still disjoint because
`d>1/24`. The constants are a declared precision policy for this preparation,
not physical constants or universal TNFR thresholds.

The shared [rate-contrast observer](../../docs/contracts/RELATIONAL_DYNAMICS.md#relational-rate-contrast)
computes `I_a/I_b-1`, retaining the denominator dependency that naive
`(I_a-I_b)/I_b` loses. Its outward dyadic bounds do not authenticate errors.
For acquisition, compare the returned endpoints with the exact rational
corner hull of the supplied intervals and require outward inflation at most
`1/1584` per side. Mathematical prediction constants also require certified
enclosures. Unresolved denominators or excess arithmetic/measurement error
mean unavailable precision, not a passing observation. Fixed absolute-grid
arithmetic and finite sensor errors do not guarantee resolution as `k->0`.

Keep both candidate predictions frozen. A report incompatible with one
candidate can exclude it only under the independently admitted premises;
overlap remains inconclusive, and incompatibility with both challenges the
two-candidate/preparation/error conjunction. No nearest-model classification
is forced. A chosen-law simulation can check this algebra, not independently
select the true constitutive law. Only the named alternatives are separated:
the larger family `F_rel+gamma*Z` has
`Q_gamma=-gamma*c/[3*(alpha+gamma*c)]`, tending to zero as positive `gamma`
tends to zero. There is no uniform separation from all mediated laws.

**Boundary of the closed gate.** The specified initial-rate contrast and
error budget are mathematically separated without a new trajectory. Temporal
sampling additionally needs the admission developed below. For a relative-phase observation
`y=theta_0-theta_2`, the shared three-point first-derivative stencil has error
`4*epsilon/h + M3*h^2/3` only with independently justified sample error and
`|y'''|<=M3` throughout each window. Both candidates and every retained
parameter value need regular-chart, horizon and derivative bounds. The P2
form-response bound cannot be transferred to this phase response. Unknown
scale cancellation at the initial jet does not bound finite-time derivatives.
No physical bridge, autonomous connection birth or new engine law follows.

The [selection controls](../../tests/physics/test_relational_exchange_selection.py)
derive this ratio from the full symbolic fields and check the bounded static
uncertainty comparison. The [observer controls](../../tests/test_relational_rate_contrast.py)
exercise interval admission, scale/sign covariance, abstention and exact
export. Neither set acquires a reserved temporal response.

### Prior-uniform temporal admission for the normalized test

<a id="capacity-discriminator-temporal-admission"></a>

**K3 phase-sampling admission v1 (2026-10-03).** The initial-rate test can
be approached by temporal samples without knowing which candidate generated
them or substituting an estimated coefficient into the truncation bound.
Both candidate laws are used to derive the common bound. This is a conditional
acquisition specification; no reserved samples are evaluated here. Supply
`e=w=1/2`, `beta in [3/4,5/4]`, the two exact preparations above, gain one,
radians and the same structural clock. These are a finite computational
reference prior and coordinate choices, not derived universal coefficients
or an admitted physical measurement map. Both named laws and both capacity
arms must satisfy the same bounds.

**Geometry and continuation.** On K3, let `j,k` be the other two nodes and
write

\[
a_i=(\theta_j+\theta_k)/2-\theta_i,\qquad
b_i=(\theta_j-\theta_k)/2.
\]

The relative phasor resultant is exactly `2*cos(b_i)*exp(i*a_i)`. In the
acute lifted chart its argument is `a_i`, so

\[
g_i=a_i/\pi,\qquad H_i=2\pi\cos(b_i)\operatorname{sinc}(a_i).
\]

This is the full nonlinear metric and pressure source, not a linearization.
Put a closed box of radius `r=1/64` in every one of the six normalized
coordinates about the exact initial state. Its edge gaps have magnitude at
most `pi/3+2*r<pi/2`, and `|a_i|<=pi/4+2*r<1`. The cosine and sinc factors
are strictly positive. The interval certificate uses mathematical-pi
enclosures, the shared trigonometric bounds and the shared sinc derivative
series, including each derivative's remainder.

For all states in these boxes and all beta in the prior, the
[static certificate](../../src/tnfr/research/relational_capacity_discriminator.py)
encloses all four complete vector fields and proves `||F||_infinity<2/5`.
The norm refers to the declared normalized coordinates. Set `T=1/32`.
Since `T*(2/5)=1/80<r`, a first-exit argument keeps the ideal trajectories
inside their boxes throughout `[0,T]`. Smoothness and continuation follow
from the positive metric on that compact chart. This proves the domain
before reading samples; a simulated path staying acute would not suffice.

**Third derivative on the whole window.** At an arbitrary interval-enclosed
point of each box, recursively evaluate the normalized Taylor coefficients
of the complete field. If `z_j` encloses `z^(j)/j!`, the recurrence is
`z_(j+1)=[F(sum z_l t^l)]_j/(j+1)`. Only orders through three are needed.
The recurrence is evaluated on the entire box, not advanced as a trajectory.
The shared interval-Taylor owner bounds the elementary functions and their
derivatives separately. Applied to `y=theta_0-theta_2`, all four cases certify

\[
|y'''|\le M_3=1/2\quad\hbox{throughout }[0,T].
\]

This bound covers both alternatives and the whole declared beta prior. It
does not reuse the earlier P2 form bound, assume phase consensus or evaluate
an error against the known source beta. Static native-field and independent
derivative controls check the K3 field representation; the certificate
retains exact outward bounds, domain margins and the admission verdict.

**Frozen sampling and error budget.** Take samples at `0,h,2h` with
`h=1/64`, so `2h=T`. Require each supplied sample of the lifted phase
difference to have total error at most `epsilon=2^-24` relative to the ideal
prepared trajectory. The initial-rate stencil then gives

\[
\widehat R=\frac{-3y_0+4y_1-y_2}{2h},\qquad
|\widehat R-R(0)|\le
\delta=\frac{4\epsilon}{h}+\frac{M_3h^2}{3}
=\frac{11}{196608}<2^{-11}.
\]

Both ideal before rates exceed `1/8` uniformly in the prior. In particular,
`k>=2/5` and `alpha>1/3` give `R_sep>2/15`; the mediated baseline is larger.
